# Fact-check: publish and docs

This log checks every factual claim in [publish-and-docs.md](../publish-and-docs.md), ADRs 0047–0050 and todos L1–L8 (L4 later split into L4a and L4b), on 2026-11-19. External facts were read from the npm registry, npm's docs and Cloudflare's docs that day; repo claims were read from `main` at `eddc212`; Lamina PDF claims from `laudebugs/laminapdf` at `7b4b37b`.

## Owner decisions

| # | Claim | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | The owner reserved the `@buildanagent` scope on npm and said "We can start publishing as soon as we're ready to" | ✅ | Owner's messages, 2026-11-19 |
| 2 | The owner chose "0.1.0 once a model call works" | ✅ | Owner's answer, 2026-11-19 |
| 3 | The owner said "Changesets wait until we publish" | ✅ | Owner's message, 2026-11-19 |
| 4 | The owner asked "We also need to build documentation for the libraries" and chose Astro Starlight | ✅ | Owner's message and answer, 2026-11-19 |
| 5 | The owner said "We'll use cloudflare to deploy docs and the website - buildanagent.fyi" and chose two sites | ✅ | Owner's message and answer, 2026-11-19 |

## npm trusted publishing

| # | Claim | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 16 | Trusted publishing needs npm CLI 11.5.1 or later and Node 22.14.0 or later | ✅ | [npm docs](https://docs.npmjs.com/trusted-publishers): "requires npm CLI version 11.5.1 or later and Node version 22.14.0 or higher" |
| 17 | It needs `id-token: write` in the workflow | ✅ | npm docs: "The critical requirement is the id-token: write permission" |
| 18 | It accepts GitHub-hosted runners only, not self-hosted ones | ✅ | npm docs: "GitHub Actions (GitHub-hosted runners)… Self-hosted runners are not currently supported" |
| 19 | It adds provenance attestations by default | ✅ | npm docs: "npm automatically generates and publishes provenance attestations… by default" |
| 20 | A trusted publisher is configured on the package's settings page, by user, repository, workflow filename and optional environment | ✅ | npm docs, "Configuring trusted publishing" |
| 21 | A new trusted publisher must complete its first publish within 2 days, or it expires | ✅ | npm docs: "must complete its first successful publish within 2 days" |
| 22 | npm may require the package to exist before a trusted publisher can be added | ⚠️ | The docs describe the setup on "your package settings", which implies an existing package, but don't say so; the plan's risk 1 and todo L4a cover both cases |
| 23 | Node 22 ships npm 10, so the release job must install npm 11.5.1 or later | ⚠️ | From the Node 22 release line's bundled npm (10.x); not checked on today's runner image. L3 installs npm explicitly either way |

| 53 | Both sites turn off `workers_dev` and still get Preview URLs | ✏️ | Previews are served on `workers.dev`, and a new Worker's `preview_urls` "initial value depends on `workers_dev`" ([Wrangler configuration](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/wrangler/configuration/), read 2026-11-19). Corrected: both `wrangler.toml` files set `preview_urls = true` (plan DS3, ADR 0049, L8) |
| 54 | A `workers.dev` Preview URL sends `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` | ✅ | [Previews](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/previews/), read 2026-11-19 |
| 55 | Vitest's `resolve.conditions` makes Node tests read `source` | ✏️ | Vitest 5.1.0 resolves Vite 8.3.8 here; Vite's `resolve.conditions` defaults to the client conditions and `ssr.resolve.conditions` to `defaultServerConditions`, `['module', 'node', 'development\|production']` ([Vite SSR options](https://vite.dev/config/ssr-options#ssr-resolve-conditions), and the installed `vite` package's export). Corrected to `ssr.resolve.conditions` with `source` before the three defaults (PB3, ADR 0050, L1) |
| 56 | `vitest/config` re-exports `defaultServerConditions` | ✏️ | It doesn't (checked by importing it, 2026-11-19), and pnpm blocks an undeclared `vite` import, so L1 spells out the list and tests it against Vite's |
| 57 | Export conditions match in object order, so `source` must precede `types` in the workspace's `exports` | ✅ | Node's packages docs: "key order is significant. During condition matching, earlier entries have higher priority" (`nodejs/node` `doc/api/packages.md`, read 2026-11-19). Corrects ADR 0050's "types first" for the workspace; "types first" stays for `publishConfig.exports` |
| 58 | Publishing sets `latest` unless `--tag` is given | ✅ | npm's `npm-dist-tag` docs: "Publishing a package sets the `latest` tag to the published version unless the `--tag` option is used" (`npm/cli` docs, read 2026-11-19) |
| 59 | A package's first publish under `--tag bootstrap` leaves `latest` unset | ⚠️ | npm's docs don't cover a package's first publish, and the registry may point `latest` at it; the plan deprecates the placeholder at once and 0.1.0 takes `latest` within 2 days |
| 60 | A tag pushed by `release.yml` with `GITHUB_TOKEN` starts the docs deploy | ✏️ | Events from `GITHUB_TOKEN` create no new runs, except that "`workflow_dispatch` and `repository_dispatch` events always create workflow runs" ([GitHub docs](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/concepts/security/github_token), read 2026-11-19). Corrected: `release.yml` dispatches `docs-deploy.yml` with the tag (DS4, L3, L8) |
| 61 | A pull request opened with `GITHUB_TOKEN` doesn't trigger `ci.yml` (row 42) | ✏️ | The same GitHub page now says such a pull request's runs start in an approval-required state, which a person with write access approves; L3's risk is updated |
| 62 | A `workflow_run` deploy can tell `main` from a pull request by its head branch | ✏️ | A fork's branch can be named `main`. Corrected: production deploys need `workflow_run.event == 'push'` and `head_repository.full_name == github.repository`, and the preview's pull request comes from the API by `head_sha`, not the artifact (DS3, ADR 0049, L8) |
| 63 | The bootstrap job can publish with provenance | ✏️ | Provenance needs the OIDC token, so the job now has `id-token: write` (PB2, ADR 0047, L3) |
| 64 | L2's criteria can all pass while the packages are private | ✏️ | With `privatePackages.version: false`, `changeset version` bumps nothing; the bump check moves to a throwaway branch with `private` removed (L2, L3) |
| 65 | B20 still owns the undocumented-export diff and a changelog test | ✏️ | L6 owns the first export diff and changesets write the changelogs; B20's scope and criteria now keep only the second diff and check for a changeset |
| 66 | One todo, L4, ships the first release | ✏️ | Its steps span more than one pull request, so L4 is split into L4a (public packages, bootstrap, trusted publishers) and L4b (0.1.0, the changeset gate, removing the bootstrap path); row 22's "todo L4" now means L4a |
| 67 | ADR 0049's index row names DS2 and DS3 | ✏️ | The ADR covers DS2 to DS4; the index row now says so |
| 68 | The plan's package versions are dated "on 2026-11-19" | ✏️ | Now "as of November 2026" with npm cited, as AGENTS.md requires |

## Checked after the owner's npm bootstrap (2026-11-19)

| # | Claim | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 69 | npm needs a package to exist before a trusted publisher can be added | ✅ | `npm/cli` `docs/lib/content/commands/npm-trust.md` at `787f152`, read 2026-11-19: "Package must exist: The package you're configuring must already exist on the npm registry." Answers risk 1, which the plan now replaces |
| 70 | Row 59: a package's first publish under `--tag bootstrap` leaves `latest` unset | ✏️ | `npm view @buildanagent/gateway dist-tags` and the same for the SDK, 2026-11-19: `latest` and `bootstrap` both point at `0.0.0-bootstrap.0`. The plan now says the first publish set `latest` |
| 71 | Each package holds `0.0.0-bootstrap.0` and `0.0.0-stage`, deprecated with "Placeholder only; install 0.1.0 or later." | ✅ | `npm view @buildanagent/<pkg> versions dist-tags deprecated --json`, 2026-11-19 |
| 72 | The placeholders carry provenance | ✏️ | The owner published them from their own machine, not a workflow, so they have none; L4a's criterion now checks versions, dist-tags and the deprecation instead |
| 73 | A `workflow_run` job in an environment limited to `main` can deploy pull-request Previews | ⚠️ | GitHub's events docs list `workflow_run`'s `GITHUB_REF` as "Default branch" (`github/docs` `content/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/events-that-trigger-workflows.md`, read 2026-11-19), so the job's ref is `main`. Whether the environment's branch rule checks that ref hasn't been tried on this repository, so L8's acceptance criteria check it |
| 74 | Until 0.1.0, `pnpm add @buildanagent/sdk` installs the deprecated placeholder | ✅ | `pnpm add @buildanagent/sdk` in a temp project, 2026-11-19: it added `0.0.0-bootstrap.0`, printed as deprecated |
| 75 | `@buildanagent/gateway/testing/setup` in `setupFiles` resolves through `ssr.resolve.conditions` (PB3) | ✏️ | Found in L1 with Vitest 5.1.0: its `resolvePath()` resolves each `setupFiles` entry at config load through `local-pkg`'s `resolveModule()`, which calls `mlly`'s `resolvePathSync()` with its default conditions `['node', 'import']` (`vitest/dist/chunks/index.Di8au7q4.js` and `nativeModuleRunner.9xPIMzi7.js` in the installed 5.1.0). With no `dist/` the entry fell back to a path under the package and every test file failed; with `dist/` it would load the built setup. Corrected in PB3: each config lists a local `test/setup.ts` that imports the setup, and Vite resolves that import through the SSR list |
| 76 | Every published `package.json` sets `sideEffects: false` (PB3) | ✏️ | Importing `@buildanagent/gateway/testing/setup` installs the offline guards (`packages/gateway/src/testing/setup.ts`), and a bundler that trusts `sideEffects: false` may drop a bare import of it. That bundler behaviour is from webpack's tree-shaking guide as remembered, unverified: `webpack.js.org` was not reachable from the L1 session ([webpack: tree shaking](https://webpack.js.org/guides/tree-shaking/)). Corrected in PB3: the gateway lists `./src/testing/setup.ts` and `./dist/testing/setup.js`, so a bundler keeps the import whichever condition it resolves; the SDK keeps `false` |
| 77 | TypeScript 7.0.2's `inlineSources` inlines sources into declaration maps too (ADR 0051) | ✏️ | Found in the L1 code review: `tsc -p packages/gateway/tsconfig.build.json --declarationMap` with `inlineSources` set wrote `index.d.ts.map` with `sources` pointing at `src/index.ts` and no `sourcesContent`, while every `.js.map` carried it. Corrected in ADR 0051: the build leaves `declarationMap` off |

## ADR 0051, checked after the low code review (2026-11-19)

Each row checks a claim in [ADR 0051](../../decisions/0051-dist-build-corrections-from-l1.md) against the L1 branch or a reproduction in it, with TypeScript 7.0.2, Vitest 5.1.0 and pnpm 10.34.9 installed.

| # | Claim | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 78 | `files: ["dist"]` drops the licences, which ADR 0050's own pack test requires | ✅ | ADR 0050's Decision sets `files: ["dist"]`, and its Consequences require the tarball to hold `LICENSE`, `LICENSE-APACHE` and `LICENSE-MIT` |
| 79 | A bundler that trusts `sideEffects: false` may drop a bare import of a side-effect file | ✅ | webpack's tree-shaking guide (`src/content/guides/tree-shaking.mdx` at webpack/webpack.js.org commit `7c4a93a`, 2026-11-18): a file "imported for its side effect alone needs to be added to the side effect list so it will not be unintentionally dropped", and an array value lists such files. This replaces row 76's remembered, unverified source |
| 80 | Vitest 5.1.0 resolves a bare `setupFiles` entry with Node's default conditions when it loads the config, so before a build it fails | ✅ | Reproduced: with the SDK's `setupFiles` set to `@buildanagent/gateway/testing/setup` and no `dist/`, both test files failed with "Cannot find module …/packages/sdk/@buildanagent/gateway/testing/setup". `resolvePath()` in the installed `vitest/dist/chunks/index.Di8au7q4.js` falls back to a path under the root when `resolveModule()` finds nothing |
| 81 | Without `inlineSources`, the build's source maps point at `../src/*.ts` | ✅ | Reproduced: `tsc -p packages/gateway/tsconfig.build.json --inlineSources false` wrote `dist/index.js.map` with `sources: ["../src/index.ts"]` and no `sourcesContent` |
| 82 | `tsc` leaves the `dist/` files of deleted modules in place | ✅ | Reproduced: a `stale.js` planted in the output folder survived a second `tsc -p packages/gateway/tsconfig.build.json` |
| 83 | Point 1: `files` lists `dist` and the three licences, `prepack` copies them with `scripts/copy-licenses.mjs`, and `.gitignore` ignores the copies | ✅ | Both `package.json` files' `files` and `prepack`; `.gitignore` lines 17–21 |
| 84 | Point 2: the gateway's `sideEffects` lists `./src/testing/setup.ts` and `./dist/testing/setup.js`; the SDK keeps `false` | ✅ | `packages/gateway/package.json` and `packages/sdk/package.json` |
| 85 | Point 3: each `setupFiles` lists `./test/setup.ts`, and Vite resolves its import to `src/` with no build | ✅ | Both `vitest.config.ts` files; `pnpm test` passed with no `packages/*/dist` present |
| 86 | Point 4: each `tsconfig.build.json` sets `sourceMap` and `inlineSources` and leaves `declarationMap` off, and TypeScript 7.0.2 inlines no sources into declaration maps | ✅ | Both `tsconfig.build.json` files. Reproduced row 77: with `--declarationMap`, `index.js.map` carried `sourcesContent` and `index.d.ts.map` did not |
| 87 | Point 5: `build` runs `scripts/clean-dist.mjs` before `tsc`, and `prepack` runs `build` before copying the licences | ✅ | Both `package.json` files' scripts. With the old gateway `prepack`, all 11 pack tests failed; with the new one they pass |
| 88 | TypeScript 7.0.2's `rewriteRelativeImportExtensions` leaves `./x.ts` in emitted `.d.ts` files | ✅ | Reproduced in a two-file project: `index.js` imported `./x.js`, while `index.d.ts` kept `export { x } from "./x.ts"` |
| 89 | The pack test checks points 1, 2, 4 and 5, and CI runs `pnpm test` before any build | ✅ | `scripts/ci/packages-pack.test.mjs` test names; `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs `pnpm test` before `node --test scripts/ci/*.test.mjs` |
| 90 | ADR 0050 stays Accepted with a dated note pointing to ADR 0051 | ✅ | ADR 0050's status block and its 2026-11-19 note |
| 91 | ADR 0050 had "four" details that don't hold and "one question open" | ✏️ | The Context listed five gaps and named no open question; the low code review added a sixth (`pnpm pack` didn't build). Corrected in ADR 0051: "five gaps", numbered, with the pack gap folded into the fifth |

## Re-checked on the live pages (2026-11-19)

Each row below re-reads on the live page a claim that earlier sessions on 2026-11-19 could check only indirectly. Those sessions couldn't reach `docs.npmjs.com`, `docs.github.com` or `webpack.js.org`, so rows 58, 69, 73, 79, 103–110, 116–119, 124, 125 and 128 cite those sites' source repositories. Row 57 likewise cites the `nodejs/node` repository rather than `nodejs.org`, and row 76 cites memory. Rows 23, 29 and 114 stayed ⚠️ with no page checked, and row 73 stayed ⚠️ because its source states less than the claim. Later that day those sites, and also `nodejs.org` and `developers.cloudflare.com`, loaded with `curl`. The original rows stay as they were, except row 118, corrected in place by row 156.

| # | Claim | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 140 | Row 103: trusted publishing needs npm CLI 11.5.1 or later and Node 22.14.0 or later | ✅ | [npm docs, trusted publishing](https://docs.npmjs.com/trusted-publishers), read 2026-11-19: "requires npm CLI version 11.5.1 or later and Node version 22.14.0 or higher" |
| 141 | Row 104: it works only on GitHub-hosted runners and needs `id-token: write` | ✅ | Same page: "GitHub Actions (GitHub-hosted runners)", "Self-hosted runners are not currently supported", "The critical requirement is the id-token: write permission" |
| 142 | Row 105: the publisher names a user, repository, workflow filename in `.github/workflows/` and an optional environment, all case-sensitive | ✅ | Same page: "The workflow file must exist in .github/workflows/ in your repository" and "All fields are case-sensitive and must be exact" |
| 143 | Row 106: a configuration created after 2026-09-03 allows only `npm stage publish` until `npm publish` is chosen | ✅ | Same page: "Configurations created after Sep 03, 2026 are automatically set to allow npm stage publish, and you can choose whether to also permit direct publishing with npm publish" |
| 144 | Row 107: provenance is automatic only via trusted publishing, from a public repository, for a public package | ✅ | Same page, "Automatic provenance generation", which lists those three conditions |
| 145 | Row 108: the package's `repository.url` must match the GitHub repository | ✅ | Same page: "your package's repository.url field in package.json must exactly match your GitHub repository" |
| 146 | Row 109: npm's docs advise "never use caching in release builds" | ✅ | Same page, the comment on `package-manager-cache: false` in both GitHub Actions examples |
| 147 | Row 110, the docs part: npm tries OIDC before any configured token | ✅ | Same page: npm "uses them for authentication before falling back to traditional tokens". The ENEEDAUTH part rests on npm 11.21.2's code, which row 110 already cites |
| 148 | Rows 69 and 128: npm needs a package to exist before a trusted publisher can be added | ✅ | [npm `trust` docs](https://docs.npmjs.com/cli/v11/commands/npm-trust), read 2026-11-19: "Package must exist: The package you're configuring must already exist on the npm registry." PB2 in the plan and CI plan §3f now cite this page instead of the `npm/cli` source |
| 149 | Row 58: publishing sets `latest` unless `--tag` is given | ✅ | [npm `dist-tag` docs](https://docs.npmjs.com/cli/v11/commands/npm-dist-tag), read 2026-11-19: "Publishing a package sets the latest tag to the published version unless the --tag option is used" |
| 150 | Row 57: export conditions match in object order | ✅ | [Node.js packages docs](https://nodejs.org/api/packages.html), read 2026-11-19: "Within the "exports" object, key order is significant. During condition matching, earlier entries have higher priority" |
| 151 | Rows 23 and 114: Node 22 ships npm 10 | ✅ | [Node.js release index](https://nodejs.org/dist/index.json), read 2026-11-19: every v22 release bundles npm 10.x, from v22.0.0 (npm 10.5.1) to v22.23.5 of 2026-11-11 (npm 10.9.9). `release.yml` installs Node from `.nvmrc` (22), so the bundled npm is too old for trusted publishing |
| 152 | Row 29: the deploy token needs Workers Scripts edit and Zone Workers Routes edit | ⚠️ | [Cloudflare API token permissions](https://developers.cloudflare.com/fundamentals/api/reference/permissions/), read 2026-11-19, lists "Workers Scripts Edit" under account permissions and "Workers Routes Edit" under zone permissions. The [GitHub Actions guide](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/ci-cd/external-cicd/github-actions/) recommends the "Edit Cloudflare Workers" template, and neither page says which permissions a custom-domain deploy needs. L8 still checks it with a real deploy |

Whether the branch rule checks that ref is a behaviour, not a page; L8 still tests it |
| 154 | Row 116: a pull request opened with `GITHUB_TOKEN` starts its `pull_request` runs in an approval-required state | ✅ | [GitHub, `GITHUB_TOKEN`](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/concepts/security/github_token), read 2026-11-19: "the resulting pull_request event creates workflow runs in an approval-required state" |
| 155 | Row 117: `workflow_dispatch` events sent with `GITHUB_TOKEN` always create runs | ✅ | Same page: "workflow_dispatch and repository_dispatch events always create workflow runs" |
| 156 | Row 118 said a `run` step without `shell:` uses `bash -e 0` | ✏️ | [GitHub, workflow syntax](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/workflow-syntax), read 2026-11-19: the unspecified shell runs `bash -e {0}`, while `shell: bash` runs `bash --noprofile --norc -eo pipefail {0}`. Row 118 had dropped the `{0}` placeholder and now reads `bash -e {0}`. The point about `pipefail` stands |
| 157 | Row 119: a job's `env:` can't read the `runner` context | ✅ | [GitHub, contexts](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/contexts), read 2026-11-19: `jobs.<job_id>.env` allows "github, needs, strategy, matrix, vars, secrets, inputs" |
| 158 | Row 124: an annotated tag's ref points at a tag object, which must be peeled to reach the commit | ✅ | [GitHub REST, Git tags](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/git/tags), read 2026-11-19: an annotated tag needs a tag object and then a `refs/tags/[tag]` reference to it, while "a lightweight tag" needs only the reference |
| 159 | Row 125: a job can wait up to 30 days for an environment approval | ✅ | [GitHub, Actions limits](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/limits), read 2026-11-19: "Gate approval time", "A workflow may wait for up to 30 days on environment approvals" |
| 160 | Rows 76 and 79: a bundler that trusts `sideEffects: false` may drop a bare import of a side-effect file | ✅ | [webpack, tree shaking](https://webpack.js.org/guides/tree-shaking/), read 2026-11-19: a CSS file "imported for its side effect alone needs to be added to the side effect list so it will not be unintentionally dropped", and "an array can be provided instead" of `false` |

## Pull request #58: the npm config fix (2026-11-19)

Rows 191–192 check what pull request #58 added to CI plan §3f and the L4b todo, against the failed run's log and local runs of npm 11.21.2.

| # | Claim | Verdict | Source and reason |

## Verified Version packages commits (2026-11-19)

Pull request #70 was blocked by "Commits must have verified signatures", so this pull request makes the version commit through GitHub's API. GitHub's claims were read on the live docs.github.com on 2026-11-19, and the repository's state through the REST API the same day.

| # | Claim | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 218 | Commits made with `createCommitOnBranch` "are automatically signed by GitHub if supported and will be marked as verified"; they are authored by the owner of the credential, and the mutation takes no author or committer | ✅ | [GraphQL reference, `createCommitOnBranch`](https://docs.github.com/en/graphql/reference/commits#mutation-createcommitonbranch), "Authorship" and "Commit Signing" |
| 219 | The mutation's commit has the branch's head as its parent, `expectedHeadOid` must match that head, and `fileChanges` holds additions and deletions | ✅ | The same page and `CreateCommitOnBranchInput` on it |
| 220 | GitHub promises a signature for commits made through APIs such as the Commits API only when the request is authenticated as a GitHub App or bot and sets no custom author, committer or signature | ✅ | [About commit signature verification](https://docs.github.com/en/authentication/managing-commit-signature-verification/about-commit-signature-verification#signature-verification-for-bots) |
| 221 | `createCommitOnBranch` called with `RELEASE_PR_TOKEN`, a fine-grained personal access token, gives a commit that reads as verified here | ⚠️ | Inferred from row 218, whose "if supported" names no condition on the token type; nothing in row 220 limits the mutation. The next release run settles it: `version-commit.mjs` fails, leaving the branch alone, unless REST reports `verification.verified` true |
| 222 | A ruleset that requires signed commits checks the head branch's commits when GitHub decides whether a pull request can merge, so an unsigned commit there blocks even a squash merge | ✅ | [Available rules for rulesets](https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/configuring-branches-and-merges-in-your-repository/managing-rulesets/available-rules-for-rulesets#require-signed-commits) |
| 223 | #70's head commit `9a375a4`, pushed with `git` by the `version-pr` job, was unsigned | ✅ | `GET repos/laudebugs/buildanagent/commits/9a375a4…` showed `verification.verified: false`, `reason: unsigned`, author `github-actions[bot]` |
| 224 | The REST git commit object carries `verification.verified` and `verification.reason` | ✅ | [REST git commits](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/git/commits) |
| 225 | §3f, `.changeset/README.md` and `CONTRIBUTING.md` said `main` didn't require the `checks` status yet | ✏️ | `GET repos/laudebugs/buildanagent/branches/main` on 2026-11-19 listed `checks` among the required contexts, enforced for non-admins; the three documents now say so |
| 226 | #70 merged at 2026-11-19T15:03:54Z as `e2b8397`, a squash commit GitHub signed, and the release run on it succeeded | ✅ | `GET repos/…/pulls/70` and `…/commits/e2b8397`, `verification.verified: true`, committer GitHub; release run 44550571848. How the owner got past the rule isn't recorded |
| 227 | Moving an open pull request's head branch back to its base would leave it with no commits, and GitHub's docs don't say what happens to it then | ⚠️ | Unverified: no docs page found. The script avoids the case with `changeset-release/staging` |

| L10-3 | The harness's `session`, `context`, `tools`, `permissions`, `sandbox`, `credentials`, `extensions`, `server` and `testing` subpaths are built, and `mcp`, `memory`, `telemetry` and `ablation` are empty stubs | ✅ | `packages/harness/src/mcp/index.ts`, `memory/index.ts`, `telemetry/index.ts` and `ablation.ts` each export `{}` with a stub comment; the other entries in `packages/harness/package.json` `exports` name modules with exports |
| L10-4 | `createContextPolicy()` builds all five context layers: write cap, masking, the summary between turns, the mid-turn summary and the overflow retry | ✏️ | Corrects the harness README's "The summary, mid-turn and overflow layers arrive in their own todos" and the `./context` module comment; `LayerName` in `packages/harness/src/context/types.ts` lists `write_cap`, `mask`, `summarize`, `mid_turn` and `overflow_retry`, and the README's own layer 3 to 5 sections describe them as built |

The claims L11 added to PB4, CI plan §3f, `.changeset/README.md`, the four package READMEs, the changeset and the review documents, checked on the branch `todo/L11-release-protocol-harness-cli` on 2026-11-22, whose base is `main` at `576f7c7`.

| # | Claim | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

The four READMEs exist at the URL pattern `https://github.com/laudebugs/buildanagent/blob/main/packages/<pkg>/README.md`, the pattern the harness README already uses for the protocol README, and `test/home.test.ts` checks the files |
| L12o-10 | The manual teaches how agents work with type-checked listings from eight SDKs | ✅ | `verify/` holds eight harnesses (claude-agent-sdk, cloudflare-agents, copilot-sdk, google-adk, langchain, openai-agents-sdk, pi, vercel-ai-sdk), and AGENTS.md says a chapter's `::listing` shows a `// #region` of a type-checked file from `verify/*/examples`; the manual lives at manual.buildanagent.fyi |

After merging main (the figure pipeline and its tests) and the round-two fixes, `pnpm --filter @buildanagent/docs test` ran 19 files, 257 tests (home.test.ts now holds 8); `astro build` reported 282 pages again after the merge |

| L12c-4 | The plugin writes the sidebar itself, one group per topic that has `items`, in its `config:setup` hook, so a TypeDoc plugin must run after it | ✅ | `dist/index.js` builds `sidebar` from each topic's `items` and calls `updateConfig({ sidebar })`; `starlight-typedoc`'s `libs/starlight.ts` `getSidebarFromReflections` returns the sidebar unchanged when `config.sidebar` is empty, and a first build with the plugins in the other order generated empty API groups and sidebars |

| L12c-6 | The `topics` option maps a topic `id` to glob patterns, so pages the sidebar does not list join that topic | ✅ | `dist/libs/config.js` option schema and `dist/middleware.js` (`picomatch(patterns)(normalizedId)` then `getTopicById`); the build passes with `topics: { "reference/gateway": ["/reference/gateway/**"], ... }` |

## L12q: the sandbox example on the fake (branch `todo/L12q-sandbox-example`, 2026-11-23)

Read from the branch `todo/L12q-sandbox-example` on top of `todo/L12q-docs-examples` at `4f462f00`. Sources are `packages/harness/src/testing/fake-sandbox.ts`, `packages/harness/src/sandbox/types.ts`, `src/sandbox/policy.ts`, the harness README's sandbox sections and the example's own test.

| ID | Claim | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L12q-11 | `fakeSandbox()` keeps its workspace in memory, enforces the policy it is given and runs no real program | ✅ | The TSDoc of `fake-sandbox.ts`: "confines no process and runs no program"; `FakeSandbox` keeps files in a `Map` and dispatches `FAKE_COMMANDS` in `run()`; the test's spy on `process.getBuiltinModule()` sees no call |
| L12q-12 | The fake reports the tier `os` and the stops `mistakes` and `exfiltration`, and the provisioned sandbox's `stops` are the same for a policy with no wildcard host and no `/` root | ✅ | `fakeSandbox()` sets `tier: "os"` and defaults `stops`; `provision()` sets `sandbox.stops` from `confirmPolicy()`, which uses `stopsFor()`; the test asserts both values for the one policy it provisions, so it does not show that another policy would change them |
| L12q-13 | A write that climbs out of the workspace (`../outside.txt` from `/work/repo`) resolves with a `filesystem_write` denial for `/work/outside.txt` instead of an exit code, and writes nothing | ✅ | `resolve()` tidies `..`; `writeCommand()` returns `{ denied }` from `writeDenial()` with the reason "the path is outside the write roots"; `ExecResult`'s denied variant in `types.ts`; asserted by the test |
| L12q-14 | The sandbox reports the policy it enforces through `readBack()`, and `provision()` has already compared it with the request and throws `SandboxPolicyMismatchError` on a difference | ✅ | `FakeSandbox.readBack()` returns a copy of its policy; `provision()` calls `confirmPolicy(client, sandbox, policy)`; the README's read-back section; the test asserts the reported policy equals the requested one |
| L12q-15 | A command's environment is exactly the `env` it is given, with nothing copied from the host | ✅ | The TSDoc of `ExecOptions.env` in `types.ts`: "The command's whole environment ... nothing else is added"; the fake's `env` command prints only `options.env` |
| L12q-16 | `srtSandbox()` and `dockerSandbox()` implement the same `provision()`, `exec()` and policy read-back | ✅ | Both return a `SandboxClient` (`types.ts`); the README's read-back section says `srtSandbox()` rereads its settings file and `dockerSandbox()` reports its mounts and flags |
| L12q-17 | Both commands reach the sandbox's record, so the denial comes from the policy and not from a command that never ran | ✅ | `exec()` pushes to the log before `run()` checks the write; the test asserts `execs: 2` |
| L12q-18 | The cap on the own-key example is per key: `answererForKey()` builds one `costGuard()` and one `memorySpend()` and its `answer()` shares them, so the first call's spend counts against the second | ✏️ | Corrects row L12q-8's reading of the earlier `answerOnUserKey()`, which built a new guard and `memorySpend()` on every call, so spend restarted at 0. `Spend`'s TSDoc and the README's "Cap each run with a fresh `Spend`" say where the `Spend` lives decides what the cap covers. The test's transport reports 900,000 output tokens (0.45 USD at the catalog's Haiku output rate of 0.5 USD per million tokens), sees the second Haiku call refused with about 0.45 USD spent and sends one request; a fresh key starts at zero |
| L12q-19 | `assertNoDefaultProvider()` is called once at startup, not on each request | ✏️ | Corrects row L12q-9's placement: the TSDoc of `src/byok/default-provider.ts` describes a global (`globalThis.AI_SDK_DEFAULT_PROVIDER`) set once, so the example calls it at module scope inside the `provider` region |
| L12q-20 | `decode()` reads a string argument as JSON text, so a framed value that is already a string must not be passed to it | ✏️ | Corrects row L12q-2's example: `decode()` in `packages/protocol/src/jsonrpc.ts` runs `JSON.parse` on a string input, so a line holding a JSON string that contains an envelope was accepted. The example now refuses a string value as `invalid_request`; the new test feeds such a line and fails without the check |
| L12q-21 | The compaction report's `replacedEntries` counts every entry before `firstKeptEntryId` on the session path (5 here, of 23 in the log), not only messages | ✏️ | Corrects row L12q-5's wording: `rebuild.ts` drops everything before `firstKeptEntryId`, and the entries include the header and turn starts; the test asserts 5 and 23 exactly |
| L12q-22 | The sandbox test proves that no process module is loaded and no host path appears, not that no Node module is ever loaded | ✏️ | Corrects row L12q-11's reading: the test spies on `process.getBuiltinModule()`, mocks `node:child_process` with a throwing factory (failing when `import "node:child_process"` is added to the example) and compares three host paths and the working folder; `node:fs` is not mocked because the test file reads it |
| L12q-23 | `assertNoDefaultProvider()` sets `globalThis.AI_SDK_DEFAULT_PROVIDER` once for the process and repeating the call does nothing | ✏️ | Corrects row L12q-19's placement: `packages/agent-safety/src/byok/default-provider.ts`: returns early when the current value is its own refusing provider, otherwise defines the read-only global; the example now calls it inside `answererForKey` instead of at import time |
| L12q-24 | The compaction example reports the latest `compaction` entry, because the next request reads that one, and throws when `firstKeptEntryId` is not in the log | ✏️ | Corrects row L12q-5's example: it reported the first compaction and could return `replacedEntries` of -1; the test chat produces one compaction, so the example's scope is one chat that compacts once, and a second compaction is not tested |
| L12q-25 | The own-key test reads the spent USD from the refusal text as a number (about 0.45 after one 900,000-token Haiku call, under 0.001 before any) | ✏️ | Corrects row L12q-18's test: it matched digit-prefix regexes; the numbers come from `budgetRefusal(error).spentUsd` as the example prints it |
| L12q-26 | The framer's default line bound is `DEFAULT_MAX_LINE_BYTES` (8 MiB); a longer line is skipped, reported once as `line_too_long` even when it arrives in two chunks, and the lines after it still read | ✅ | `packages/protocol/src/framer.ts` (`#add()` sets `#skipping`, `#finishLine()` drops the skipped line); `readStream()` passes no `maxLineBytes`, so the new test sends a line 10 bytes over the default, and fails when the example drops `line_too_long` from `refused` |
| L12q-27 | A line that ends in CRLF reads like one that ends in LF, through `readStream()` and `summarizeSession()` | ✅ | `#finishLine()` in `framer.ts` drops one trailing `\r` before parsing; `summarizeSession()` splits on `\n` and `JSON.parse` accepts the left-over `\r` as whitespace. The two new tests pass; only the stream test exercises the framer's handling, and removing the example's handling was not tried for the file test, which relies on `parseEntry()` alone |
| L12q-28 | The default compaction budget is the model's window less 16,384 tokens, at most 200,000 | ✅ | `RESERVE_TOKENS`, `MAX_BUDGET_TOKENS` and `Math.min(window - RESERVE_TOKENS, MAX_BUDGET_TOKENS)` in `packages/harness/src/context/policy.ts`; the README's layer 3 section says the same |
| L12q-29 | The compaction report describes the latest compaction, and the test covers only one compaction | ✅ | `reportOn()` uses `findLast()`; `test/examples/compaction.test.ts` runs three questions, so exactly one compaction happens; a chat with two compactions is not tested |

## L12l: Markdown twins (branch `todo/L12l-docs-markdown-twins`, 2026-11-23)

Read on 2026-11-23 from llmstxt.org, MDN, the WebKit blog, the Astro and Starlight docs, and the installed Astro 7.4.11 and Starlight 0.42.10 type definitions. The measured build numbers are from `pnpm --filter @buildanagent/docs build` on this branch.

| ID | Claim | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L12l-1 | `llms.txt` is Markdown with one H1 (the only required part), then a blockquote summary, then H2 sections of `- [name](url): notes` lists; an H2 named `Optional` holds links an agent can skip when it needs a shorter context | ✅ | llmstxt.org, "Format": the section order, the list-entry form and the Optional convention |
| L12l-2 | `new ClipboardItem({ "text/plain": promise })` accepts a promise that resolves to a Blob or a string | ✅ | MDN, `ClipboardItem()` constructor: each value may be a `Blob`, a string or a `Promise` that resolves to either; its example passes `fetch(...).then(r => r.blob())` |
| L12l-3 | A clipboard write must be triggered during a user gesture (a click or touch handler), and a `ClipboardItem` takes a promise per MIME type, so the data may arrive after the gesture | ✅ | WebKit blog, "Async Clipboard API": "The request to write to the clipboard must be triggered during a user gesture"; each item is "initialized with a mapping of MIME type to Promise" |
| L12l-4 | The resolved Blob's type must equal its MIME key, so the button rebuilds the text as a `text/plain` blob | ⚠️ | The WebKit post says the promise resolves to a string or a Blob "of the same MIME type", but neither it nor MDN states what a mismatch does. The code takes the safe reading; no browser was run |
| L12l-5 | A `<link rel="alternate" type="text/markdown" href="…">` in a page's head is a convention for pointing at the page's Markdown version | ⚠️ | No primary specification found. `rel="alternate"` with a `type` is HTML's way to link another representation of a document, but no standard names `text/markdown` for it; the docs use it as a convention and a test requires it |
| L12l-6 | At Astro 7.4.11 the `astro:build:done` hook receives `{ pages, dir: URL, assets, logger }`, and `dir` is the client build output, so a file written there is served | ✅ | The installed `astro/dist/types/public/integrations.d.ts` and the Astro integrations reference, "astro:build:done"; the built `dist/` holds the twins |
| L12l-7 | A Starlight plugin's `config:setup` hook receives the user config, including `sidebar`, and an `addIntegration` function | ✅ | The installed `@astrojs/starlight/dist/utils/plugins.d.ts` (the `addIntegration` signature and its documented example); the built site's `llms.txt` follows the sidebar |
| L12l-8 | Starlight's `components.PageTitle` replaces the page title, the default is `@astrojs/starlight/components/PageTitle.astro`, and our `PageTitle.astro` renders the default component and reads the page's address from `Astro.locals.starlightRoute.id` | ✅ | The installed `schemas/components.js` (the `PageTitle` default) and `components/PageTitle.astro`; this PR's `src/components/PageTitle.astro` imports the default and calls `twinHref(Astro.locals.starlightRoute.id)`; the Starlight docs, "Overriding components" |
| L12l-9 | The splash template (the docs home) renders no `PageTitle`, so the home page has the head link and no button | ✅ | Measured: the built `dist/index.html` has the alternate link and no `twin-copy` button; every other built page has both |
| L12l-10 | The twins are 282 files (13 written by hand, 269 generated), 515 KiB in all, written in about 0.5 s of a 14 s build; `llms-full.txt` is 558 KB | ✅ | Measured on this branch from the build's log line and `wc -c` of `dist/`; the generated pages are 492 KB of it |
| L12l-11 | Cloudflare's assets server serves a `.md` file with a Markdown or plain-text type | ⚠️ | Not verified: no deploy was run, and Cloudflare's docs were not read for the type it guesses. The twins and the button work with either type, since the button reads the body as text |
| L12l-12 | Starlight leaves a page with `draft: true` out of a production build | ✅ | The installed `@astrojs/starlight/dist/utils/routing/index.js` line 24: a page is kept when `MODE !== "production"` or `data.draft === false` |
| L12l-13 | Astro's content loader slugs each path segment with github-slugger, which lower-cases, removes what its regex lists and then turns each space into `-`, and a `slug` in the front matter replaces the id | ✅ | github-slugger 2.0.0 `index.js` lines 75–76 (`toLowerCase`, `replace(regex, '')`, `replace(/ /g, '-')`); the installed Astro `content/utils.js` lines 20–21 (`if (data.slug) return String(data.slug)`) |
| L12l-14 | Row L12l-9 said every page but home has the link and button; the 404 page's `starlightRoute.id` is `404` and it has no source file in the content folder | ✏️ | Corrects row L12l-9's claim that every page but the home page has the head link and the button: the built `404.html` had the link to a twin that is not written. The rule now rejects `404`, and the built test reads every `.html` file, the 404 page included |
| L12l-15 | An MDX string-literal expression leaves the string's text in the twin | ✏️ | Corrects the README's claim: the text is backslash-escaped for `\ * _ [ ] < > # ~`, the pipe and the backtick, because Markdown would otherwise read `{'*'}` as emphasis while the page shows the character. Measured by `test/twins-flatten.test.ts` |
| L12l-16 | The button's blob should have the type of its key, `text/plain`; what a mismatch does is not documented | ✏️ | Corrects the README and the `copy-markdown.ts` header, which said "must" on the strength of row L12l-4, still ⚠️: neither the WebKit post nor MDN says what a mismatch does. The code rebuilds the text as `text/plain` to stay on the documented side |

## L12e: the docs version tag, banner and install-line test (branch `todo/L12e-docs-version-banner`, 2026-11-23)

| # | Claim | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L12e-1 | With `--noindex`, `site-stage-dist.mjs` sets `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` on every path: it writes main's `public/_headers` with the header added to its one `/*` rule, or, when the app has none, `NOINDEX_HEADERS`; apps/docs has no `public/_headers` today, so the file is those two lines | ✅ | `scripts/ci/site-stage-dist.mjs` lines 11 to 13 (header comment), line 55 (`NOINDEX_HEADERS`) and lines 68 to 72 (`headersFor`); `apps/docs/public/` holds only `favicon.svg` and `fonts/` |
| L12e-2 | `site-guard-deploy.mjs --noindex` fails the deploy when `dist/_headers` differs from what the stage script writes | ✅ | `scripts/ci/site-guard-deploy.mjs` lines 95 to 121 (`checkSite`: `deployed !== expected` adds the problem) |
| L12e-3 | `docs-deploy.yml`'s `next` job stages and guards with `--noindex`, and the `preview` job does the same | ✅ | `.github/workflows/docs-deploy.yml` lines 417 to 420 (`next`) and 468 to 471 (`preview`) |
| L12e-4 | A `workers.dev` Preview URL carries `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` from Cloudflare | ✅ | [Previews](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/previews/), read 2026-11-23: "To prevent search engines from indexing your Preview, use its `workers.dev` URL, which includes an `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` response header"; the page recommends Cloudflare Access for custom-domain Previews. The custom-domain half is row L12e-25 |
| L12e-5 | Starlight 0.42.10 has a `Banner` component slot, rendered at the top of `<main>` on every page, whose default shows the page's `banner` front matter | ✅ | Installed `@astrojs/starlight/dist/schemas/components.d.ts` (`Banner?: string`, "Banner component rendered at the top of each page"), `dist/components/Page.astro` line 96 (`<Banner />` inside `<main>`) and `dist/components/Banner.astro` (`banner && <div class="sl-banner" …>`) |
| L12e-6 | The front matter `banner` cannot depend on the host, because its content is fixed at build time | ✅ | `dist/components/Banner.astro` renders `Astro.locals.starlightRoute.entry.data.banner.content` with `set:html`; the value comes from the page's own front matter at build time |
| L12e-7 | `location.hostname` holds the host name without the port | ✅ | [MDN, Location: hostname](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Location/hostname), read 2026-11-23: its example shows `anchor.hostname === "developer.mozilla.org"` for a URL with port 4097 |
| L12e-8 | `location.hostname` may end in a dot for a fully qualified name, and browsers resolve `*.localhost` to the loopback address | ⚠️ | Not verified: MDN's page does not mention a trailing dot or `.localhost`. The rule strips one trailing dot and accepts `next.localhost` regardless, so the code does not depend on either claim; the README says only that `next.localhost` shows the banner |
| L12e-9 | Astro bundles the script's import into one inline `<script type="module">` whose text is the same on every page | ✅ | Measured on the built `404.html`: the module holds the two functions and the call `t(document.querySelectorAll(...), location.hostname)`; `test/version-built.test.ts` requires one such script per page with identical text |
| L12e-10 | The SDK's version is `0.1.0-alpha.34`, and the six packages version as one fixed group | ✅ | `packages/sdk/package.json` line "version"; AGENTS.md and `.changeset/config.json` (fixed group) |
| L12e-11 | Starlight's banner rules apply only to its own `Banner` component, so an element that merely carries the `sl-banner` class gets no accent colours, padding or link colour | ✏️ | Corrects the README, the `Banner.astro` header comment and the claim in the version notes that the banner "reuses the `sl-banner` class for the accent colours". `dist/components/Banner.astro` in Starlight 0.42.10 scopes `.sl-banner` and `.sl-banner a` with Astro's scope class, so the built CSS selector carries `:where(.astro-…)` that our div lacks. Only `print.css` (`.sl-banner`, unscoped) applies to our element. `Banner.astro` now styles `.unreleased-banner` itself, and `test/version-built.test.ts` reads the built CSS for it |
| L12e-12 | The banner's colours are the measured pair "a banner on the accent" (page background on the accent, text), and its focus ring is page background on the accent (3:1) | ✅ | `src/styles/starlight.css` maps `--sl-color-text-invert` to `--color-background` and `--sl-color-bg-accent` to `--color-accent`; `src/design/pairs.ts` holds both pairs, and `test/contrast.test.ts` measures them in both modes |
| L12e-13 | The footer's "Docs for main" link leads to a live page only after the stable switch, so it is shown only once the SDK version is stable | ✏️ | Corrects the README and the L12e outcome, which said the link appeared on every host and led to a host with no deploy. Per DS4 in `docs/plans/publish-and-docs.md` (line 121 and the versioning design) `next.docs` deploys only from the first stable tag. `mainDocsLinkFor` in `src/chrome.ts` returns the link only for the stable rule |
| L12e-14 | The footer sentence "describe the unreleased code on the main branch" misled while docs.buildanagent.fyi serves main | ✏️ | Reworded to "follow the main branch, so they can describe changes that no release has yet", which is true on both hosts and shown only after the switch |
| L12e-15 | The install-line command list now covers `pnpm i`, `pnpm install`, `npm add`, `yarn dlx`, `bun add` and `bunx` beside the earlier six | ⚠️ | Not verified against each tool's CLI docs in this fix: the list is a set of command names the rule matches, taken from the maintainer's finding, not a claim about what each command does. The fixtures in `test/install-lines.test.ts` run each name |
| L12e-16 | A subpath such as `@buildanagent/gateway/testing` is an import specifier, not an install argument | ⚠️ | Not verified against npm's docs in this fix. It is the rule the test defines (stated in the README), and it follows the maintainer's finding; the fixtures pin it |
| L12e-17 | A prerelease other than `-alpha.` has no install-line rule yet, so `installRuleFor` throws | ✏️ | Corrects the earlier test, which asserted that `0.2.0-beta.1` and `1.0.0-rc.1` use the stable rule. Only the `alpha` dist-tag exists (AGENTS.md, release.yml publishes under `alpha`), so a beta or rc would need its own tag rule; the function now throws a message naming `installRuleFor` |
| L12e-18 | The "Docs for main" footer paragraphs sit after Starlight's `</footer>`, not inside it | ✅ | Measured on a built page: `…</div></footer><p class="license…`; Starlight's `Footer.astro` ends its `<footer class="sl-flex">` before our override's markup. The test therefore reads the region after the last `</footer>` |
| L12e-19 | On a `next.` host the banner can shift the layout, because the module script un-hides it after first paint | ⚠️ | Not re-read at MDN in this fix: module scripts are deferred, so the page can paint first; the banner is `hidden` in the HTML (checked by the test). Documented as a known limitation in the README, with a synchronous head script as the fix; not built, since a second script breaks the one-hash CSP design (L12m) and `next.` is not live |
| L12e-20 | Expressive Code leaves `>` unescaped inside a copy button's `data-code="..."` attribute | ✅ | Measured on built pages in `apps/docs/dist`: `data-code="…(_, i) => &#x60;Refund rule…"` carries a raw `=>`, while quotes and backticks are entities. A tag pattern of `<[^>]*>` therefore ends at the first `>` and leaks the rest of the attribute into the text; the pattern is now quote-aware and a fixture holds `2>&1` |
| L12e-21 | Expressive Code closes every line with `</div></div>`, so a rendered continuation line ends in a backslash and two line breaks | ✅ | Measured on a built bash block: `<div class="ec-line"><div class="code">…</div></div>` per line. The earlier join consumed only one newline, so a continuation in built HTML was never joined; the join now takes any run of line breaks |
| L12e-22 | The earlier statement that a continuation joins "a backslash and the next line" held for source Markdown only | ✏️ | Corrects the README and rule comment: in built text the continuation spans a run of line breaks, and the test covers both shapes |
| L12e-23 | A package suffix of any form (`@^0.1.0`, `@~1`, `@>=0.1`, `@*`, an exact version) is an install target to check; under the alpha rule only exactly `@alpha` passes | ✅ | Behaviour of `src/version/install-lines.ts`, pinned by fixtures. The strict reading of a pinned `-alpha.N` is a decision, stated in the README: it names a release that the next alpha supersedes, so the line would go stale |
| L12e-24 | The banner's text is in the HTML of every page on every host, hidden by the `hidden` attribute and `data-pagefind-ignore` | ✅ | Read in `Banner.astro` and checked by `test/version-built.test.ts`; a crawler or reader without JavaScript can see the text on docs. and next., and Pagefind skips it |
| L12e-25 | A custom-domain Worker such as next.docs gets no `X-Robots-Tag` header from Cloudflare | ⚠️ | Not verified at a Cloudflare page: Previews documents `workers.dev` only. The claim is read from the stage script's own comment (`scripts/ci/site-stage-dist.mjs`; see also row L8-7) |
| L12e-26 | While only alphas exist, npm's `latest` tag serves a placeholder, not a release | ✅ | `npm view @buildanagent/sdk dist-tags`, run 2026-11-23: `latest: '0.0.0-bootstrap.0'`, `bootstrap: '0.0.0-bootstrap.0'`, `alpha: '0.1.0-alpha.34'`; `apps/docs/src/content/docs/start/install.mdx` says the same |

## The protocol and CLI pages (L12g, 2026-11-26)

| # | Claim | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L12g-1 | A client opens with `initialize`, whose params are `clientInfo` (a name and a version) and `capabilities` (a list of strings), and `encode()` checks the envelope and ends the line with a newline | ✅ | `packages/protocol/src/messages.ts` (`ClientCommand`), `src/jsonrpc.ts` (`encode()`); `test/examples.test.ts` checks the exact line `initializeLine()` writes |
| L12g-2 | `readInitializeAnswer()` returns the server's `InitializeResult` and throws on an error answer, on a stream with no answer and on a line that is not a message | ✅ | `packages/protocol/examples/quick-start.ts`; three tests in `packages/protocol/test/examples.test.ts` |
| L12g-3 | `buildanagent rpc` refuses to start without `--model` and the provider's key in the environment, and `initialize` makes no model call | ✅ | Ran it with a cleared environment: exit 2 with "reads its key from ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, which is not set"; `packages/cli/test/bin.test.ts` answers `initialize` with a key that is not real; the transcript's command now sets `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=not-a-real-key` itself, so a reader who pastes it gets the same answer |
| L12g-4 | The `initialize` answer names the server and the SDK version, lists capabilities and the two experimental methods, and `pending` is empty when no request waits | ✅ | `packages/cli/transcripts/rpc-initialize.txt`, recorded from the binary and compared by `packages/cli/test/transcripts.test.ts`; `InitializeResult` in `messages.ts` |
| L12g-5 | The transcript shows `<version>` for the SDK version, which the CLI test substitutes before it compares | ✅ | `withoutVersion()` in `packages/cli/test/transcripts.test.ts` replaces `SDK_VERSION`, imported from `@buildanagent/sdk`; `dispatcher.ts` reads the same constant |
| L12g-6 | A client sends `initialize`, then `session/create` or `session/open`, then `turn/start`, and waits for a response before a dependent command, because the server may answer out of order | ✅ | `packages/cli/README.md` (the `rpc` section) and `packages/protocol/README.md` ("Commands run concurrently"); the order matches `examples/client-requests.ts` |
| L12g-7 | `turn/start` answers `{ accepted: true, turn }` or `{ accepted: false, reason }` with `busy`, `locked` or `invalid`, and each accepted turn ends with `turn/settled` | ✅ | `Disposition` in `packages/protocol/src/messages.ts`; `SERVER_NOTIFICATION_METHODS` in `jsonrpc.ts`; the protocol README's "Turns" bullet |

## The docs' content security policy (L12m, 2026-11-26)

Checked against Cloudflare's `_headers` page for Workers static assets, MDN's CSP reference and its browser-compat-data (`http/headers/Content-Security-Policy.json` on `main`, read 2026-11-26), the WHATWG HTML and W3C CSP3 specifications, the installed Astro 7.4.11 and Starlight 0.42.10, and a build of `todo/L12m-docs-csp`.

| # | Claim | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L12m-1 | The owner answered yes to question 9b, "Strict CSP", on 2026-11-26 | ✅ | Secondhand: the coordinating session's brief for L12m relays the owner's answer of 2026-11-26, and the L12 todo's owner questions (9b) record it; no message from the owner is in the repository |
| L12m-2 | Cloudflare allows at most 2,000 characters on one `_headers` line, spacing, header name and value included | ✅ | developers.cloudflare.com/workers/static-assets/headers/, "Limits": "Each line in the `_headers` file has a 2,000 character limit" |
| L12m-3 | Cloudflare allows up to 100 header rules | ✅ | Same page, "Limits": "You may define up to 100 header rules" |
| L12m-4 | Cloudflare's asset server reads `_headers` and never serves the file itself | ✅ | Same page: "This file will not itself be served as a static asset"; a local `wrangler dev` (Wrangler 4.160.1) answered `/_headers` with 404 |
| L12m-5 | A `_headers` rule is a path line followed by indented `Name: value` lines, and `#` starts a comment | ✅ | Same page, the format section and its example |
| L12m-6 | The `/*` rule's headers reach pages, Markdown twins and the 404 page that `not_found_handling = "404-page"` serves | ⚠️ | Seen locally only: `wrangler dev` with the committed file sent the header on `/start/install/` (200), `/start/install.md` and `/nope/` (404). Cloudflare's page does not say whether a rule applies to a not-found response, and nothing was deployed |
| L12m-7 | The deploy writes main's `apps/docs/public/_headers` into the staged site, never the artifact's own, and adds `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` to its one `/*` rule for a preview or `next.` | ✅ | `headersFor()` and `stageSiteZip()` in `scripts/ci/site-stage-dist.mjs`; `previewHeaders()` in `apps/manual/scripts/stage-dist.mjs`, which refuses more than one `/*` rule or an `X-Robots-Tag` of the file's own |
| L12m-8 | The guard fails a deploy whose `dist/_headers` differs from what the stage script writes, and allows a top-level `_headers` entry in the artifact | ✅ | `checkSite()` in `scripts/ci/site-guard-deploy.mjs`; `ALLOWED_UNDERSCORE` and `checkName()` in `apps/manual/scripts/stage-dist.mjs` |
| L12m-9 | A preview and a release deploy both stage main's `_headers`: every deploy job checks out `main` before it stages, and a release builds its tag's commit | ✅ | `.github/workflows/docs-deploy.yml`: the `checkout-main` anchor (`ref: main`) that every deploy job reuses, and the header comment on `workflow_dispatch` with a `tag` |
| L12m-10 | Astro 7 has a `security.csp` option that adds a `<meta http-equiv="content-security-policy">` element to each page | ✅ | `security.csp` in Astro 7.4.11's `dist/types/public/config.d.ts`: "When enabled, Astro will add a `<meta>` element inside the `<head>` element of each page" |
| L12m-11 | Astro's documentation says its CSP does not support Shiki's inline styles | ✅ | Same comment: "Shiki isn't currently supported. By design, Shiki functions use inline styles that cannot work with Astro CSP implementation" |
| L12m-12 | A policy delivered in a `<meta>` element cannot set `frame-ancestors` | ✅ | W3C CSP Level 3, "The `<meta>` element" section: `frame-ancestors`, `report-uri` and `sandbox` are not supported in a policy delivered by `<meta>` |
| L12m-13 | A hash source names the base64 SHA-256 (or SHA-384 or SHA-512) digest of an inline script's exact text, whitespace included | ✅ | MDN, `script-src`: "CSP supports sha256, sha384 and sha512" and "capitalization and whitespace matter"; CSP3's "Does element match source list for type and source?" algorithm hashes the element's text as UTF-8. `test/csp.test.ts` checks the empty-string vector `47DEQpj8…uFU=` |
| L12m-14 | An HTML parser turns CR LF and lone CR into LF before it reads a script's text, so the build normalizes line ends before it hashes | ✅ | WHATWG HTML, "Preprocessing the input stream" in the parsing section: newlines are normalized before tokenization |
